Owner design rules in force (handoff section "Design decisions in force"): the coordinator owns the single persisted projection, updated incrementally, never rebuilt on restart; every first-attempt test failure is a real defect, never re-run past; existing tests are the specification unless a ruling names the class. - **Workspace.** `/code/workspace` is root-owned. Prefer `scripts/test.bash --remote --test <selector>` (runs on the buildtest service, results land locally exactly like a local run). When you must run locally, wrap it: `flock /tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/local-slot.lock scripts/test.bash --local ...` (blocking flock, no -n/-w) and log wait/acquire times in your findings. Do NOT set JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS for BuildTestEmbedded. Never run a whole suite unless your brief names it. - **Another orchestrator session is also working this campaign** (from a different machine). Before pushing to any branch, `git fetch` and confirm the remote head is what you started from; push with `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<expected-sha>` only. If someone else moved your target branch, STOP and report — never overwrite their push. - **Test-driven.** For behavior changes the first pushed milestone is a test failing for the stated reason (an assertion, never a compile error), recorded in findings; then the fix; then revert the product change, rerun, record red again (reversal control). Read https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/DocumentationRepository/blob/main/architecture/TESTING.md (sections "Test-Driven Development", "Never Calibrate a Reproducer to a Machine", "Tests Must Be Hermetically Sealed") before writing tests. Count test results in *attempts*: kompile retries failed tests; read the native XML `<attempt-history>` / per-test attempt lines and quote attempt counts — a pass after a retry is a failure. - **Mechanism first.** Before proposing any change write "the mechanism is X, and the evidence that demonstrates X is Y". Treat timeout, retry, fallback, graceful degradation as red flags. "The host was overloaded" is never a root cause. NEVER raise a timeout, deadline or bound; NEVER reduce an iteration count; never delete, skip, or weaken a test or assertion. Every edit to an existing test is listed (before/after/justification) in findings and the PR, and only where the brief authorizes it. **Fixture corrections are pre-authorized** (also for reviewer proofs adopted into the PR — they are PR tests, not main tests) when ALL hold: the failing test's assertions and bounds stay byte-identical; the change only makes the fixture own an ordering/readiness signal it previously left to chance, through an existing public seam (callback, latch, future, acknowledged write); you first show the race deterministically (force the losing order, record red) and then green; and you ledger it (test, before, after, mechanism). A correction that weakens, removes, retimes or re-targets an assertion is NOT a fixture correction and needs a ruling — STOP for it. - **Not yours:** the handoff record itself — never `claim`, `report`, `heartbeat`, `release`, `update` or `complete` the campaign handoff (the orchestrator owns it and your `release` would drop its claim); read it only. Never invoke the report-challenge CLI (it merges). Your progress goes in your findings file and your branches, nothing else. - **Not yours:** merging, enqueueing, `gh pr merge`, `--admin`, publishing artifacts to kotlin.directory, bumping pins in other repos, deploying, restarting production services. Production is read-only. The one exception you may take without asking: truncating a log file that is demonstrably filling the disk (keep the tail if it holds evidence; say so). - **PRs** target `main` only, open as DRAFT, and the description opens with why the change exists (the provenance given in your brief), then what it does, the test evidence (first-attempt counts), and the ledger of any existing-test edits. Never reference a PR by bare number — full URLs. Commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; PR descriptions end with `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)`. Update PR bodies with `gh api -X PATCH repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n> -f body=@file` style REST calls (`gh pr edit` fails silently on these repos) and read back. Never chain a rebase and a force-push in one command. - **Progress.** Append to your findings file (path in your brief) every ~5 minutes: one paragraph, OBSERVED facts marked apart from INFER. Push a checkpoint commit to your `wip/` branch at every milestone starting with the first; push evidence (logs, XML excerpts, findings copy) to a `wip/<lane>-evidence` branch. If killed now, a fresh lane must be able to resume from your last push. - **Waits.** Run long commands as background jobs you wait on by PID with a hard deadline that recognises every terminal state; never `until grep …; sleep` on text you have not seen printed; never `pgrep -f` a string in your own command line. Before your final message kill every background process you started and confirm with `ps -eo pid,args -ww`. Do not watch CI — stop once pushed; the orchestrator holds the remote wait. - **Exit without delivering is allowed.** If the premise is wrong, say so with evidence — that is a complete result. Wall-clock limit is from launch, waits included. - **Lifecycle, ownership and concurrency work.** Your brief carries an owner/lock-order table written by the orchestrator. Treat it as the specification: every public callback or observer point is listed with the owners held there and the journal/engine operations it may invoke synchronously; a condition wait is legal only while holding owners the awaited producer never needs; the order is enforced at ONE acquisition choke point that fails loudly with a descriptive error naming both owners, never re-established per call site. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Coverage is derived from that table — one proof per row — never from a hand list of the failures you happen to know; after any supplied callback returns, re-check close before the next I/O, including inside helpers; a failed admission releases every token it registered, including on errors after the wrapper returns. - **Scope.** One pull request = one feature, one invariant, or one behavior. Split by feature complexity, never by line count — each PR is a self-contained chunk that can land on its own and leaves the system incrementally better (owner, 2026-10-18: "You want self-contained chunks which can be landed and which make things incrementally better"): a change that carries more than one concern (an ownership change plus performance fixes plus a test-lookup rewrite; a new commit path plus a lock-order redesign) is a sequence of independently landing PRs, each with its own reviews. If the work you are asked for carries more than one concern, STOP after the first landable concern: push it, open its PR, and name the remaining concerns in order — never widen a PR to carry a second one. (Owner, 2026-10-18.) - **Traversals.** When a brief's rule applies to a tree walk, recursion or retry chain, enforce it at every node the traversal visits, at the one traversal point — never only at the root or first step. Before coding, list in the findings every site that visits or yields a node, so coverage is derived from that list. - **State machines: participants first.** When a brief carries a lifecycle/state table, list in your findings before coding every participant: ownership/scope releases that can throw, catches after a commit boundary, helper loops that follow a callback, and every writer of durable state (including recovery and legacy workers). Each must be covered by a row; an uncovered participant is a STOP naming it. - **No sub-delegates.** A lane never spawns another model or agent (no luna, astra, opus or nested codex). Review reading, source audit and test writing are the lane's own work.  ## Scope freeze (owner, 2026-10-18 21:58 UTC — "why so few merges") A PR lands the rows already proven in it. A new gap, participant or row found during implementation or review becomes a follow-up PR (recorded in the description under "Follow-ups (separate PRs)" with its evidence), unless it is a P1 defect in this PR's own code. Coverage-only review findings (P2/P3 "add this proof") are adopted and the PR lands; they do not open a new review round. Only a P1 against the PR's own behavior re-opens a review, and that review covers the delta of the final head. A PR past round three is split at its next completed row, not extended. Clarification (22:50 UTC): the scope freeze defers NEW rows and gaps. It never defers a test that is in this PR's `tests/` and red: such a proof is fixed (fixture correction, assertions byte-identical) or removed from the PR to its evidence branch before any push; a PR's own tests are always first-attempt green at the pushed head.  ## Final line discipline (orchestrator, 2026-10-18 23:08 UTC) A lane that pushes a head may write the final line `PR <url> …` or `PUSHED <sha> …` only after it has audited that head's own CI run (every shard's native XML: canonical = highest = 1 for owned tests; zero hard failures; retried neighbours listed). If the wall limit arrives before the run finishes, the final line is `PUSHED <sha> ; CI audit pending run <id>` and the orchestrator holds reviews until the audit. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Coverage is derived from that table — one proof per row — never from a hand list of the failures you happen to know; after any supplied callback returns, re-check close before the next I/O, including inside helpers; a failed admission releases every token it registered, including on errors after the wrapper returns. - **Scope.** One pull request = one feature, one invariant, or one behavior. If the work you are asked for carries more than one concern, STOP after the first landable concern: push it, open its PR, and name the remaining concerns in order — never widen a PR to carry a second one. (Owner, 2026-10-18.) - **Traversals.** When a brief's rule applies to a tree walk, recursion or retry chain, enforce it at every node the traversal visits, at the one traversal point — never only at the root or first step. Before coding, list in the findings every site that visits or yields a node, so coverage is derived from that list. - **State machines: participants first.** When a brief carries a lifecycle/state table, list in your findings before coding every participant: ownership/scope releases that can throw, catches after a commit boundary, helper loops that follow a callback, and every writer of durable state (including recovery and legacy workers). Review reading, source audit and test writing are the lane's own work.  ## Scope freeze (owner, 2026-10-18 21:58 UTC — "why so few merges") A PR lands the rows already proven in it. Every edit to an existing test is listed (before/after/justification) in findings and the PR, and only where the brief authorizes it. **Fixture corrections are pre-authorized** (also for reviewer proofs adopted into the PR — they are PR tests, not main tests) when ALL hold: the failing test's assertions and bounds stay byte-identical; the change only makes the fixture own an ordering/readiness signal it previously left to chance, through an existing public seam (callback, latch, future, acknowledged write); you first show the race deterministically (force the losing order, record red) and then green; and you ledger it (test, before, after, mechanism). A correction that weakens, removes, retimes or re-targets an assertion is NOT a fixture correction and needs a ruling — STOP for it. - **Not yours:** the handoff record itself — never `claim`, `report`, `heartbeat`, `release`, `update` or `complete` the campaign handoff (the orchestrator owns it and your `release` would drop its claim); read it only. Never invoke the report-challenge CLI (it merges). Your progress goes in your findings file and your branches, nothing else. - **Not yours:** merging, enqueueing, `gh pr merge`, `--admin`, publishing artifacts to kotlin.directory, bumping pins in other repos, deploying, restarting production services. Production is read-only. The one exception you may take without asking: truncating a log file that is demonstrably filling the disk (keep the tail if it holds evidence; say so). - **PRs** target `main` only, open as DRAFT, and the description opens with why the change exists (the provenance given in your brief), then what it does, the test evidence (first-attempt counts), and the ledger of any existing-test edits. Push a checkpoint commit to your `wip/` branch at every milestone starting with the first; push evidence (logs, XML excerpts, findings copy) to a `wip/<lane>-evidence` branch. Wall-clock limit is from launch, waits included. - **Lifecycle, ownership and concurrency work.** Your brief carries an owner/lock-order table written by the orchestrator. Treat it as the specification: every public callback or observer point is listed with the owners held there and the journal/engine operations it may invoke synchronously; a condition wait is legal only while holding owners the awaited producer never needs; the order is enforced at ONE acquisition choke point that fails loudly with a descriptive error naming both owners, never re-established per call site. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Split by feature complexity, never by line count — each PR is a self-contained chunk that can land on its own and leaves the system incrementally better (owner, 2026-10-18: "You want self-contained chunks which can be landed and which make things incrementally better"): a change that carries more than one concern (an ownership change plus performance fixes plus a test-lookup rewrite; a new commit path plus a lock-order redesign) is a sequence of independently landing PRs, each with its own reviews. The owner's goal, verbatim: "maximize droplet utilization as close to 100% as possible as per https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/BuildTestEmbedded/blob/main/docs/UTILIZATION_ARCHITECTURE.md and fix any slow projections by fixing underlying root causes as described in https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/DocumentationRepository/blob/main/playbooks/SLOW_SERVICES.md#fix-1-persist-expensive-projections-update-them-incrementally also https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/DocumentationRepository/blob/main/architecture/STATE_MANAGEMENT.md". Owner design rules in force (handoff section "Design decisions in force"): the coordinator owns the single persisted projection, updated incrementally, never rebuilt on restart; every first-attempt test failure is a real defect, never re-run past; existing tests are the specification unless a ruling names the class. - **Workspace.** `/code/workspace` is root-owned. Clone fresh into the NEW directory named in your brief (under `/tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/lanes/workspace/`). Never reuse another checkout. Run `gh auth setup-git` and set `git config user.name codexcoder21` / `user.email codexcoder21@users.noreply.github.com` in your clone before committing. Read the repo's README.md and AGENTS.md first. - **Shared machine.** 8 CPUs, a 6 GiB memory ceiling shared by up to six lanes. Prefer `scripts/test.bash --remote --test <selector>` (runs on the buildtest service, results land locally exactly like a local run). When you must run locally, wrap it: `flock /tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/local-slot.lock scripts/test.bash --local ...` (blocking flock, no -n/-w) and log wait/acquire times in your findings. Do NOT set JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS for BuildTestEmbedded. Never run a whole suite unless your brief names it. - **Another orchestrator session is also working this campaign** (from a different machine). Before pushing to any branch, `git fetch` and confirm the remote head is what you started from; push with `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<expected-sha>` only. If someone else moved your target branch, STOP and report — never overwrite their push. - **Test-driven.** For behavior changes the first pushed milestone is a test failing for the stated reason (an assertion, never a compile error), recorded in findings; then the fix; then revert the product change, rerun, record red again (reversal control). Read https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/DocumentationRepository/blob/main/architecture/TESTING.md (sections "Test-Driven Development", "Never Calibrate a Reproducer to a Machine", "Tests Must Be Hermetically Sealed") before writing tests. Count test results in *attempts*: kompile retries failed tests; read the native XML `<attempt-history>` / per-test attempt lines and quote attempt counts — a pass after a retry is a failure. - **Mechanism first.** Before proposing any change write "the mechanism is X, and the evidence that demonstrates X is Y". Treat timeout, retry, fallback, graceful degradation as red flags. "The host was overloaded" is never a root cause. NEVER raise a timeout, deadline or bound; NEVER reduce an iteration count; never delete, skip, or weaken a test or assertion. Every edit to an existing test is listed (before/after/justification) in findings and the PR, and only where the brief authorizes it. **Fixture corrections are pre-authorized** (also for reviewer proofs adopted into the PR — they are PR tests, not main tests) when ALL hold: the failing test's assertions and bounds stay byte-identical; the change only makes the fixture own an ordering/readiness signal it previously left to chance, through an existing public seam (callback, latch, future, acknowledged write); you first show the race deterministically (force the losing order, record red) and then green; and you ledger it (test, before, after, mechanism). A correction that weakens, removes, retimes or re-targets an assertion is NOT a fixture correction and needs a ruling — STOP for it. - **Not yours:** the handoff record itself — never `claim`, `report`, `heartbeat`, `release`, `update` or `complete` the campaign handoff (the orchestrator owns it and your `release` would drop its claim); read it only. Never invoke the report-challenge CLI (it merges). Your progress goes in your findings file and your branches, nothing else. - **Not yours:** merging, enqueueing, `gh pr merge`, `--admin`, publishing artifacts to kotlin.directory, bumping pins in other repos, deploying, restarting production services. Production is read-only. The one exception you may take without asking: truncating a log file that is demonstrably filling the disk (keep the tail if it holds evidence; say so). - **PRs** target `main` only, open as DRAFT, and the description opens with why the change exists (the provenance given in your brief), then what it does, the test evidence (first-attempt counts), and the ledger of any existing-test edits. Never reference a PR by bare number — full URLs. Commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; PR descriptions end with `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)`. Update PR bodies with `gh api -X PATCH repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n> -f body=@file` style REST calls (`gh pr edit` fails silently on these repos) and read back. Never chain a rebase and a force-push in one command. - **Progress.** Append to your findings file (path in your brief) every ~5 minutes: one paragraph, OBSERVED facts marked apart from INFER. Push a checkpoint commit to your `wip/` branch at every milestone starting with the first; push evidence (logs, XML excerpts, findings copy) to a `wip/<lane>-evidence` branch. If killed now, a fresh lane must be able to resume from your last push. - **Waits.** Run long commands as background jobs you wait on by PID with a hard deadline that recognises every terminal state; never `until grep …; sleep` on text you have not seen printed; never `pgrep -f` a string in your own command line. Before your final message kill every background process you started and confirm with `ps -eo pid,args -ww`. Do not watch CI — stop once pushed; the orchestrator holds the remote wait. - **Exit without delivering is allowed.** If the premise is wrong, say so with evidence — that is a complete result. Wall-clock limit is from launch, waits included. - **Lifecycle, ownership and concurrency work.** Your brief carries an owner/lock-order table written by the orchestrator. Treat it as the specification: every public callback or observer point is listed with the owners held there and the journal/engine operations it may invoke synchronously; a condition wait is legal only while holding owners the awaited producer never needs; the order is enforced at ONE acquisition choke point that fails loudly with a descriptive error naming both owners, never re-established per call site. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Coverage is derived from that table — one proof per row — never from a hand list of the failures you happen to know; after any supplied callback returns, re-check close before the next I/O, including inside helpers; a failed admission releases every token it registered, including on errors after the wrapper returns. - **Scope.** One pull request = one feature, one invariant, or one behavior. Split by feature complexity, never by line count — each PR is a self-contained chunk that can land on its own and leaves the system incrementally better (owner, 2026-10-18: "You want self-contained chunks which can be landed and which make things incrementally better"): a change that carries more than one concern (an ownership change plus performance fixes plus a test-lookup rewrite; a new commit path plus a lock-order redesign) is a sequence of independently landing PRs, each with its own reviews. If the work you are asked for carries more than one concern, STOP after the first landable concern: push it, open its PR, and name the remaining concerns in order — never widen a PR to carry a second one. (Owner, 2026-10-18.) - **Traversals.** When a brief's rule applies to a tree walk, recursion or retry chain, enforce it at every node the traversal visits, at the one traversal point — never only at the root or first step. Before coding, list in the findings every site that visits or yields a node, so coverage is derived from that list. - **State machines: participants first.** When a brief carries a lifecycle/state table, list in your findings before coding every participant: ownership/scope releases that can throw, catches after a commit boundary, helper loops that follow a callback, and every writer of durable state (including recovery and legacy workers). Each must be covered by a row; an uncovered participant is a STOP naming it. - **No sub-delegates.** A lane never spawns another model or agent (no luna, astra, opus or nested codex). Review reading, source audit and test writing are the lane's own work.  ## Scope freeze (owner, 2026-10-18 21:58 UTC — "why so few merges") A PR lands the rows already proven in it. A new gap, participant or row found during implementation or review becomes a follow-up PR (recorded in the description under "Follow-ups (separate PRs)" with its evidence), unless it is a P1 defect in this PR's own code. Coverage-only review findings (P2/P3 "add this proof") are adopted and the PR lands; they do not open a new review round. Only a P1 against the PR's own behavior re-opens a review, and that review covers the delta of the final head. A PR past round three is split at its next completed row, not extended. Clarification (22:50 UTC): the scope freeze defers NEW rows and gaps. It never defers a test that is in this PR's `tests/` and red: such a proof is fixed (fixture correction, assertions byte-identical) or removed from the PR to its evidence branch before any push; a PR's own tests are always first-attempt green at the pushed head.  ## Final line discipline (orchestrator, 2026-10-18 23:08 UTC) A lane that pushes a head may write the final line `PR <url> …` or `PUSHED <sha> …` only after it has audited that head's own CI run (every shard's native XML: canonical = highest = 1 for owned tests; zero hard failures; retried neighbours listed). An expired activation window plus a stale per-id 404 observed BEFORE eligibility is not absence; recovery after a backward clock step issues DELETE (or re-reads) and only then confirms. The contract is the unchanged inherited test's specification (`restartRecoveryAcrossBackwardClockStepWaitsOneActivationWindowForNeverVisibleDroplet` asserts exactly one DELETE for a never-visible droplet) and it stands: a droplet that was never observed present is confirmed absent only by an acknowledged DELETE — a provider that has not yet listed a droplet still being created can answer 404 to a read while the machine goes on to exist, so a read can never confirm absence for the never-observed class, whereas DELETE is idempotent and its acknowledgement (success or not-found) is. A droplet that WAS observed present may be confirmed absent by a post-eligibility not-found read or by an acknowledged DELETE, as row F2 says. Write this distinction into your own invariant table in the PR description (observed-present vs never-observed, and what confirms each), and make the single confirmation point enforce it. If you find the inherited test or the README contradicts this, stop and say exactly where.  ## Continuation (orchestrator, 2026-10-19 01:10 UTC) — fleetobs34 died on a provider overload after both proofs were red Resume from `/tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/out/fleetobs34-findings.md` and its evidence branch `wip/fleetobs34-evidence`: the F2 forced-order proof is red at 940e6488 (run a7704a47; exact DELETE assertion expected [1] actual []), the F5 retention proof is red (run bceb220a; three stale identities retained after sibling deletion and an empty refresh — observation-only paths never join the index maps, so maintenance never visits them). Owner design rules in force (handoff section "Design decisions in force"): the coordinator owns the single persisted projection, updated incrementally, never rebuilt on restart; every first-attempt test failure is a real defect, never re-run past; existing tests are the specification unless a ruling names the class. - **Workspace.** `/code/workspace` is root-owned. Clone fresh into the NEW directory named in your brief (under `/tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/lanes/workspace/`). Never reuse another checkout. Run `gh auth setup-git` and set `git config user.name codexcoder21` / `user.email codexcoder21@users.noreply.github.com` in your clone before committing. Read the repo's README.md and AGENTS.md first. - **Shared machine.** 8 CPUs, a 6 GiB memory ceiling shared by up to six lanes. Prefer `scripts/test.bash --remote --test <selector>` (runs on the buildtest service, results land locally exactly like a local run). When you must run locally, wrap it: `flock /tmp/claude-1000/-code/2e1aa1b4-104e-4f23-9d8b-414b02ecb20f/scratchpad/local-slot.lock scripts/test.bash --local ...` (blocking flock, no -n/-w) and log wait/acquire times in your findings. Do NOT set JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS for BuildTestEmbedded. Never run a whole suite unless your brief names it. - **Another orchestrator session is also working this campaign** (from a different machine). Before pushing to any branch, `git fetch` and confirm the remote head is what you started from; push with `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<expected-sha>` only. If someone else moved your target branch, STOP and report — never overwrite their push. - **Test-driven.** For behavior changes the first pushed milestone is a test failing for the stated reason (an assertion, never a compile error), recorded in findings; then the fix; then revert the product change, rerun, record red again (reversal control). Read https://github.com/CodexCoder21Organization/DocumentationRepository/blob/main/architecture/TESTING.md (sections "Test-Driven Development", "Never Calibrate a Reproducer to a Machine", "Tests Must Be Hermetically Sealed") before writing tests. Count test results in *attempts*: kompile retries failed tests; read the native XML `<attempt-history>` / per-test attempt lines and quote attempt counts — a pass after a retry is a failure. - **Mechanism first.** Before proposing any change write "the mechanism is X, and the evidence that demonstrates X is Y". Treat timeout, retry, fallback, graceful degradation as red flags. "The host was overloaded" is never a root cause. NEVER raise a timeout, deadline or bound; NEVER reduce an iteration count; never delete, skip, or weaken a test or assertion. Every edit to an existing test is listed (before/after/justification) in findings and the PR, and only where the brief authorizes it. **Fixture corrections are pre-authorized** (also for reviewer proofs adopted into the PR — they are PR tests, not main tests) when ALL hold: the failing test's assertions and bounds stay byte-identical; the change only makes the fixture own an ordering/readiness signal it previously left to chance, through an existing public seam (callback, latch, future, acknowledged write); you first show the race deterministically (force the losing order, record red) and then green; and you ledger it (test, before, after, mechanism). A correction that weakens, removes, retimes or re-targets an assertion is NOT a fixture correction and needs a ruling — STOP for it. - **Not yours:** the handoff record itself — never `claim`, `report`, `heartbeat`, `release`, `update` or `complete` the campaign handoff (the orchestrator owns it and your `release` would drop its claim); read it only. Never invoke the report-challenge CLI (it merges). Your progress goes in your findings file and your branches, nothing else. - **Not yours:** merging, enqueueing, `gh pr merge`, `--admin`, publishing artifacts to kotlin.directory, bumping pins in other repos, deploying, restarting production services. Production is read-only. The one exception you may take without asking: truncating a log file that is demonstrably filling the disk (keep the tail if it holds evidence; say so). - **PRs** target `main` only, open as DRAFT, and the description opens with why the change exists (the provenance given in your brief), then what it does, the test evidence (first-attempt counts), and the ledger of any existing-test edits. Never reference a PR by bare number — full URLs. Commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; PR descriptions end with `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)`. Update PR bodies with `gh api -X PATCH repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n> -f body=@file` style REST calls (`gh pr edit` fails silently on these repos) and read back. Never chain a rebase and a force-push in one command. - **Progress.** Append to your findings file (path in your brief) every ~5 minutes: one paragraph, OBSERVED facts marked apart from INFER. Push a checkpoint commit to your `wip/` branch at every milestone starting with the first; push evidence (logs, XML excerpts, findings copy) to a `wip/<lane>-evidence` branch. If killed now, a fresh lane must be able to resume from your last push. - **Waits.** Run long commands as background jobs you wait on by PID with a hard deadline that recognises every terminal state; never `until grep …; sleep` on text you have not seen printed; never `pgrep -f` a string in your own command line. Before your final message kill every background process you started and confirm with `ps -eo pid,args -ww`. Do not watch CI — stop once pushed; the orchestrator holds the remote wait. - **Exit without delivering is allowed.** If the premise is wrong, say so with evidence — that is a complete result. Wall-clock limit is from launch, waits included. - **Lifecycle, ownership and concurrency work.** Your brief carries an owner/lock-order table written by the orchestrator. Treat it as the specification: every public callback or observer point is listed with the owners held there and the journal/engine operations it may invoke synchronously; a condition wait is legal only while holding owners the awaited producer never needs; the order is enforced at ONE acquisition choke point that fails loudly with a descriptive error naming both owners, never re-established per call site. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Coverage is derived from that table — one proof per row — never from a hand list of the failures you happen to know; after any supplied callback returns, re-check close before the next I/O, including inside helpers; a failed admission releases every token it registered, including on errors after the wrapper returns. - **Scope.** One pull request = one feature, one invariant, or one behavior. Split by feature complexity, never by line count — each PR is a self-contained chunk that can land on its own and leaves the system incrementally better (owner, 2026-10-18: "You want self-contained chunks which can be landed and which make things incrementally better"): a change that carries more than one concern (an ownership change plus performance fixes plus a test-lookup rewrite; a new commit path plus a lock-order redesign) is a sequence of independently landing PRs, each with its own reviews. If the work you are asked for carries more than one concern, STOP after the first landable concern: push it, open its PR, and name the remaining concerns in order — never widen a PR to carry a second one. (Owner, 2026-10-18.) - **Traversals.** When a brief's rule applies to a tree walk, recursion or retry chain, enforce it at every node the traversal visits, at the one traversal point — never only at the root or first step. Before coding, list in the findings every site that visits or yields a node, so coverage is derived from that list. - **State machines: participants first.** When a brief carries a lifecycle/state table, list in your findings before coding every participant: ownership/scope releases that can throw, catches after a commit boundary, helper loops that follow a callback, and every writer of durable state (including recovery and legacy workers). Each must be covered by a row; an uncovered participant is a STOP naming it. - **No sub-delegates.** A lane never spawns another model or agent (no luna, astra, opus or nested codex). Review reading, source audit and test writing are the lane's own work.  ## Scope freeze (owner, 2026-10-18 21:58 UTC — "why so few merges") A PR lands the rows already proven in it. A new gap, participant or row found during implementation or review becomes a follow-up PR (recorded in the description under "Follow-ups (separate PRs)" with its evidence), unless it is a P1 defect in this PR's own code. Coverage-only review findings (P2/P3 "add this proof") are adopted and the PR lands; they do not open a new review round. Only a P1 against the PR's own behavior re-opens a review, and that review covers the delta of the final head. A PR past round three is split at its next completed row, not extended. Clarification (22:50 UTC): the scope freeze defers NEW rows and gaps. It never defers a test that is in this PR's `tests/` and red: such a proof is fixed (fixture correction, assertions byte-identical) or removed from the PR to its evidence branch before any push; a PR's own tests are always first-attempt green at the pushed head.  ## Final line discipline (orchestrator, 2026-10-18 23:08 UTC) A lane that pushes a head may write the final line `PR <url> …` or `PUSHED <sha> …` only after it has audited that head's own CI run (every shard's native XML: canonical = highest = 1 for owned tests; zero hard failures; retried neighbours listed). If the wall limit arrives before the run finishes, the final line is `PUSHED <sha> ; CI audit pending run <id>` and the orchestrator holds reviews until the audit. The one exception you may take without asking: truncating a log file that is demonstrably filling the disk (keep the tail if it holds evidence; say so). - **PRs** target `main` only, open as DRAFT, and the description opens with why the change exists (the provenance given in your brief), then what it does, the test evidence (first-attempt counts), and the ledger of any existing-test edits. Never reference a PR by bare number — full URLs. Commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; PR descriptions end with `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)`. Update PR bodies with `gh api -X PATCH repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n> -f body=@file` style REST calls (`gh pr edit` fails silently on these repos) and read back. Never chain a rebase and a force-push in one command. - **Progress.** Append to your findings file (path in your brief) every ~5 minutes: one paragraph, OBSERVED facts marked apart from INFER. Push a checkpoint commit to your `wip/` branch at every milestone starting with the first; push evidence (logs, XML excerpts, findings copy) to a `wip/<lane>-evidence` branch. If killed now, a fresh lane must be able to resume from your last push. - **Waits.** Run long commands as background jobs you wait on by PID with a hard deadline that recognises every terminal state; never `until grep …; sleep` on text you have not seen printed; never `pgrep -f` a string in your own command line. Before your final message kill every background process you started and confirm with `ps -eo pid,args -ww`. Do not watch CI — stop once pushed; the orchestrator holds the remote wait. - **Exit without delivering is allowed.** If the premise is wrong, say so with evidence — that is a complete result. Wall-clock limit is from launch, waits included. - **Lifecycle, ownership and concurrency work.** Your brief carries an owner/lock-order table written by the orchestrator. Treat it as the specification: every public callback or observer point is listed with the owners held there and the journal/engine operations it may invoke synchronously; a condition wait is legal only while holding owners the awaited producer never needs; the order is enforced at ONE acquisition choke point that fails loudly with a descriptive error naming both owners, never re-established per call site. One hostile-caller public-API test per table row, red-first. If a row is missing or wrong, STOP and say which — do not improvise an order. Coverage is derived from that table — one proof per row — never from a hand list of the failures you happen to know; after any supplied callback returns, re-check close before the next I/O, including inside helpers; a failed admission releases every token it registered, including on errors after the wrapper returns. - **Scope.** One pull request = one feature, one invariant, or one behavior. Split by feature complexity, never by line count — each PR is a self-contained chunk that can land on its own and leaves the system incrementally better (owner, 2026-10-18: "You want self-contained chunks which can be landed and which make things incrementally better"): a change that carries more than one concern (an ownership change plus performance fixes plus a test-lookup rewrite; a new commit path plus a lock-order redesign) is a sequence of independently landing PRs, each with its own reviews. If the work you are asked for carries more than one concern, STOP after the first landable concern: push it, open its PR, and name the remaining concerns in order — never widen a PR to carry a second one. (Owner, 2026-10-18.) - **Traversals.** When a brief's rule applies to a tree walk, recursion or retry chain, enforce it at every node the traversal visits, at the one traversal point — never only at the root or first step. Before coding, list in the findings every site that visits or yields a node, so coverage is derived from that list. - **State machines: participants first.** When a brief carries a lifecycle/state table, list in your findings before coding every participant: ownership/scope releases that can throw, catches after a commit boundary, helper loops that follow a callback, and every writer of durable state (including recovery and legacy workers). Each must be covered by a row; an uncovered participant is a STOP naming it. - **No sub-delegates.** A lane never spawns another model or agent (no luna, astra, opus or nested codex). Review reading, source audit and test writing are the lane's own work.  ## Scope freeze (owner, 2026-10-18 21:58 UTC — "why so few merges") A PR lands the rows already proven in it. A new gap, participant or row found during implementation or review becomes a follow-up PR (recorded in the description under "Follow-ups (separate PRs)" with its evidence), unless it is a P1 defect in this PR's own code. Coverage-only review findings (P2/P3 "add this proof") are adopted and the PR lands; they do not open a new review round. Only a P1 against the PR's own behavior re-opens a review, and that review covers the delta of the final head. A PR past round three is split at its next completed row, not extended. Clarification (22:50 UTC): the scope freeze defers NEW rows and gaps. It never defers a test that is in this PR's `tests/` and red: such a proof is fixed (fixture correction, assertions byte-identical) or removed from the PR to its evidence branch before any push; a PR's own tests are always first-attempt green at the pushed head.  ## Final line discipline (orchestrator, 2026-10-18 23:08 UTC) A lane that pushes a head may write the final line `PR <url> …` or `PUSHED <sha> …` only after it has audited that head's own CI run (every shard's native XML: canonical = highest = 1 for owned tests; zero hard failures; retried neighbours listed).
